**P2 - Iterative improvement**5:T1d0e,#### **Description**

Currently, the `llm_judge` evaluator provides a binary `PASS`/`FAIL` outcome. This is effective for clear-cut cases but doesn't capture the nuance of Large Language Model (LLM) responses, which can often be partially correct, contain good information but have poor formatting, or be conceptually right but incomplete.

This ticket proposes an enhancement to the `llm_judge` evaluator to support a multi-level evaluation scale (e.g., `FAIL`, `PARTIAL PASS`, `PASS`). This will enable more granular and realistic testing of LLM behavior, providing more insightful feedback on an agent's performance. It allows scenario creators to define what constitutes a full pass versus a partial one, leading to more sophisticated agent evaluations.

#### **Acceptance Criteria**

1.  The `LLMJudgeEvaluationSchema` in `packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts` is updated to allow the `expected` field to be an array of strings, representing the possible evaluation levels (e.g., `['FAIL', 'PARTIAL', 'PASS']`).
2.  The `LLMJudgeEvaluator` in `packages/cli/src/scenarios/EvaluationEngine.ts` is refactored to instruct the LLM judge to respond with one of the provided evaluation levels.
3.  The `EvaluationResult` interface is updated to include the specific `level` (e.g., 'PARTIAL') returned by an evaluator, in addition to the overall `success` boolean.
4.  The `Reporter` class in `packages/cli/src/scenarios/Reporter.ts` is updated to display the evaluation level in its output, using distinct icons and colors for each level (e.g., ✅ PASS, 🟠 PARTIAL, ❌ FAIL).
5.  The final `judgment` logic in `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts` is enhanced to accommodate the new levels. For example, an `all_pass` strategy should require all evaluations to achieve the highest success level (e.g., 'PASS').
6.  The process exit code logic remains `0` for an overall scenario pass and `1` for a fail, based on the final judgment.

#### **Technical Approach**

**1. Update Scenario Schema (`schema.ts`)**

Modify the `LLMJudgeEvaluationSchema` to accept an array of strings for the `expected` field. This array defines the custom evaluation scale for the judge. The system prompt for the LLM will be dynamically constructed from this array.

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts
// ...
const LLMJudgeEvaluationSchema = BaseEvaluationSchema.extend({
  type: z.literal('llm_judge'),
  prompt: z.string(),
  // Change from z.string() to z.array(z.string()) to define the evaluation scale.
  // Default to a binary scale if not provided.
  expected: z.array(z.string()).optional().default(['FAIL', 'PASS']),
});
// ...
```

**2. Refactor Evaluation Engine (`EvaluationEngine.ts`)**

The `LLMJudgeEvaluator` must be updated to pass the new evaluation scale to the LLM. The general `EvaluationResult` type will also be updated to include the `level`.

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/EvaluationEngine.ts
// ...
export interface EvaluationResult {
    success: boolean; // True if not the lowest evaluation level
    level: string;    // The specific outcome, e.g., 'PASS', 'FAIL', 'PARTIAL'
    message: string;
}

interface Evaluator {
    // This method will now return the string level of the outcome.
    evaluate(runtime: IAgentRuntime, result: ScenarioResult): Promise<string>;
    getMessage(level: string): string;
    // Helper to get the expected levels
    getLevels(): string[];
}

class LLMJudgeEvaluator implements Evaluator {
    constructor(private prompt: string, private expected: string[]) {}

    async evaluate(runtime: IAgentRuntime, result: ScenarioResult): Promise<string> {
        const systemPrompt = `You are an AI assistant that judges the output of a command. Based on the prompt and the command output, respond with ONLY one of the following values: [${this.expected.join(', ')}].`;

        const llmResult = await runtime.useModel('TEXT_LARGE', {
            system: systemPrompt,
            messages: [{
                role: 'user',
                content: `Prompt: ${this.prompt}\nOutput: ${result.stdout}`
            }]
        });
        
        const response = llmResult.trim().toUpperCase();
        // Validate the LLM's response against the expected levels.
        if (this.expected.map(e => e.toUpperCase()).includes(response)) {
            return response;
        }
        // Default to the first (lowest) level if the LLM's response is invalid.
        return this.expected[0].toUpperCase();
    }
    
    getLevels(): string[] {
        return this.expected;
    }
    // ... getMessage remains similar ...
}

export class EvaluationEngine {
    // ...
    async run(runtime: IAgentRuntime, result: ScenarioResult): Promise<EvaluationResult[]> {
        const results: EvaluationResult[] = [];
        for (const evaluator of this.evaluators) {
            const level = await evaluator.evaluate(runtime, result);
            const levels = evaluator.getLevels();
            // "Success" is defined as any outcome that is not the lowest possible level.
            const success = level !== levels[0].toUpperCase();
            results.push({ success, level, message: evaluator.getMessage(level) });
        }
        return results;
    }
}
```

**3. Enhance Reporter (`Reporter.ts`)**

Update the reporter to handle and display the new evaluation levels with distinct formatting.

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/Reporter.ts
// ...
  public reportEvaluationResults(results: EvaluationResult[]) {
    // ...
    results.forEach(res => {
      let status;
      // Use a switch to handle different levels, with default for unknown levels.
      switch(res.level.toUpperCase()) {
        case 'PASS':
          status = chalk.green('✅ PASS');
          break;
        case 'PARTIAL':
        case 'PARTIAL PASS':
          status = chalk.yellow('🟠 PARTIAL');
          break;
        case 'FAIL':
        default:
          status = chalk.red('❌ FAIL');
          break;
      }
      console.log(`${status}: ${res.message}`);
    });
    // ...
  }
// ...
```

**4. Update Judgment Logic (`scenario.ts`)**

The logic for determining the final outcome must be updated to be aware of the different levels.

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts
// ...
// --- JUDGMENT ---
if (scenario.judgment?.pass?.all) {
    // Strictest strategy: all evaluations must be the highest possible level.
    finalStatus = evalResults.every(res => res.level === 'PASS'); // Assuming 'PASS' is the highest
}
// Potentially add new strategies here in the future, e.g., 'all_pass_or_partial'
// ...
```

#### **Testing Strategy**

1.  **Create a new scenario file**: `llm-judge-partial-pass.scenario.yaml`.
2.  **Define a multi-level evaluation**:
    ```yaml
    # ...
    evaluations:
      - type: llm_judge
        prompt: "Respond with the capital of France in a complete sentence."
        expected: ['FAIL', 'PARTIAL', 'PASS']
    judgment:
      pass:
        all: true # This will require a 'PASS' result
    ```
3.  **Run with an input that yields a partial pass** (e.g., `input: "echo 'Paris'"`).
    *   **Verify**: The reporter shows `🟠 PARTIAL`, the final status is `❌ FAIL`, and the exit code is `1`.
4.  **Run with an input that yields a full pass** (e.g., `input: "echo 'The capital of France is Paris.'"`).
    *   **Verify**: The reporter shows `✅ PASS`, the final status is `✅ PASS`, and the exit code is `0`.6:T495,## Description

Investigate and resolve the 3-second MCP plugin initialization delay on Vercel due to WebSocket handshake requirements.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Understand root cause of 3-second delay
- [ ] Research options: Redis cache, Gateway pattern, or dedicated server
- [ ] Implement solution that keeps connection alive
- [ ] Test performance improvement

## Technical Notes

**From today's meeting (Ben):**

* MCP plugin takes 3 seconds to initialize on every message
* Caused by needing to establish WebSocket communication with server
* Vercel serverless functions don't maintain state between invocations
* Options discussed:
  1. Redis cache for connection state (but WebSocket still needs reconnect)
  2. Gateway pattern like Discord (dedicated server for stateful connections)
  3. Accept the delay for now, optimize later

**Shaw's guidance:**

* For any WebSocket service, use the Gateway pattern similar to Discord
* "It's not like everything has to be serverless" - just avoid idle pods doing nothing
* Gateway service can scale, handles WebSocket → turns into HTTP to agent

## Assignee

Ben

**P0 - Blocking developer productivity**b:T495,## Problem

As autonomous agents become more capable, they need the ability to transact with each other - pay for services, data, or collaboration. Currently there's no standard way for Eliza agents to:

- Hold and manage wallets (USD/crypto)
- Transfer funds to other agents
- Use escrow for task completion
- Track revenue and expenses

## Proposed Solution

We've been building [AURA Infra](https://nanilabs.io) - payment infrastructure specifically designed for AI agents. We'd love to contribute a plugin that enables:

1. **Agent Wallets** - Each Eliza agent gets a wallet with USD balance
2. **Instant Transfers** - Send/receive payments between agents (2.9% fee)
3. **Escrow System** - Lock funds until task completion, then auto-release
4. **Revenue Tracking** - Dashboard for agent earnings/spending

## Why This Matters

The agent economy is exploding. Moltbook has 1.5M+ agents, agent tokens are launching, and real money is flowing. Infrastructure providers will win.

## Demo

Try it now (no signup): https://nanilabs.io/playground.html
API Docs: https://api.nanilabs.io/docs

Happy to contribute a PR or discuss integration. What do you think?

## Problem Statement

* **Cost**: Spending thousands of dollars monthly on Max 4.6
* **Inefficiency**: Using brute force Max 4.6 for everything may not create the best code
* **Lack of Guidance**: No clear process for when to use which model
* **Model Strengths**: Each model has different strengths that we're not leveraging optimally

## Model Analysis Needed

### Max Mode (Opus 4.6)

* **When it helps**: Specific, narrow scope tasks where depth is needed
* **When it doesn't help**:
  * Broader scope tasks (goes beyond requirements)
  * Creates unnecessary amounts of code that needs to be trimmed back
  * Over-engineers solutions

### Normal Mode

* **Better for**: Making things functional without over-engineering
* **Advantage**: More focused, less unnecessary code

### Composer

* **Strengths**: Super fast
* **Considerations**: Might not be as smart, but there are instances where it performs better
* **Use cases**: Need to identify when Composer is optimal

### Other Models (Codex, etc.)

* **Planning**: Which model is best for planning tasks?
* **Execution**: Which model is best for code execution?
* **Bug Fixing**: Which model is best for debugging and fixing bugs?

## Deliverables

1. **Process Documentation**: Create a comprehensive guide on when to use which model
2. **Decision Framework**: Clear criteria for model selection based on task type
3. **Cost Optimization**: Strategies to reduce unnecessary Max 4.6 usage
4. **Best Practices Guide**: Latest and greatest practices for each model
5. **Team Education**: Educate the team on optimal model usage
6. **Iterative Improvement**: Process to refine and improve the guidance over time

## Success Metrics

* Reduced monthly costs on Max 4.6
* Improved code quality
* Faster development cycles
* Better understanding of model strengths/weaknesses across the team

* This should be an ongoing process that evolves as we learn more

- checkShouldRespond defaults to true but can be turned off for ChatGPT mode1a:T1cd9,## Summary

Fixes critical runtime errors in `plugin-bootstrap` providers when metadata or values are null/undefined.

## Problem

The agent crashes with the error:
```
Object.entries requires that input parameter not be null or undefined
```

This occurs in several providers:
1. **relationshipsProvider**: When `entity.metadata` is null/undefined
2. **actionStateProvider**: When `result.values` or `workingMemory` are not objects
3. **recentMessagesProvider**: Similar issues with null values

## Solution

Added proper null/undefined checks before calling `Object.entries()`:

### 1. `src/providers/relationships.ts`
- Added null/undefined check in `formatMetadata()`
- Returns `'{}'` for null/undefined metadata instead of crashing

### 2. `src/providers/actionState.ts`  
- Added type check for `result.values` before calling `Object.entries()`
- Added type/null check for `workingMemory` before calling `Object.keys()`

## Changes

- **packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/relationships.ts**: Guard `formatMetadata()` against null metadata
- **packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/actionState.ts**: Add type guards for `result.values` and `workingMemory`

## Testing

1. Started ElizaOS agent
2. Sent messages via web interface (http://localhost:3000)
3. Verified no more `Object.entries` errors
4. Confirmed agent responds properly instead of showing IGNORE action

## Impact

- ✅ Prevents agent crashes when entities have null metadata
- ✅ Improves stability for action result processing
- ✅ Fixes "IGNORE" action issue when agent can't retrieve conversation context
- ✅ No breaking changes - only adds safety checks

Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR fixes critical `Object.entries` runtime errors in plugin-bootstrap providers that caused agent crashes when metadata or values were null/undefined. However, **the PR contains significantly more changes than described in the title and description**.

## What's Actually in This PR

### 1. Plugin-Bootstrap Fixes (Matches PR Description)
- `actionState.ts`: Added type guards before `Object.entries()` on `result.values` and `workingMemory`
- `relationships.ts`: Added null check in `formatMetadata()` to prevent crashes

### 2. Major New Feature (Not Mentioned in PR Description)
- **Request Context System**: New per-entity settings infrastructure for multi-tenant deployments
  - Added `packages/core/src/request-context.ts` and `request-context.node.ts` (856+ lines)
  - Modified `runtime.ts` `getSetting()` to check request context first
  - Enables different users sharing the same runtime to have different API keys, OAuth tokens, etc.

### 3. Message Service Changes (Not Mentioned in PR Description)
- Refactored message creation logic in `message.ts`
- Added `MESSAGE_SENT` event emission after sending to central server

### 4. Version Bumps
- All packages bumped to `1.7.3-alpha.3`

## Concerns

The PR title says "fix: Add null/undefined checks" but this PR includes:
- A major architectural feature (request context system)
- Message service refactoring
- 30 files changed, 1107 insertions, 52 deletions

**This should have been split into separate PRs** for better review, testing, and rollback capability. The plugin-bootstrap fixes are straightforward and safe, but bundling them with a major new feature makes it difficult to:
- Review each change independently
- Test each feature in isolation
- Roll back if issues arise with one component

## Technical Review

The actual code changes are well-implemented:
- Null checks are correctly placed and handle edge cases
- Request context system follows AsyncLocalStorage patterns appropriately
- Message service changes maintain event emission order

The plugin-bootstrap fixes will definitely prevent the `Object.entries` crashes described in the PR.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

- This PR contains well-implemented code but has significant scope creep beyond its stated purpose
- Score of 3 reflects that while the code quality is good and the plugin-bootstrap fixes are safe, the PR includes undocumented major features (request context system, message service changes) that should have been separate PRs. This makes comprehensive testing difficult and increases risk. The PR description is misleading about the actual scope of changes.
- Pay close attention to `packages/core/src/runtime.ts` and `packages/core/src/request-context.ts` as these introduce a new architectural pattern for per-entity settings that affects how settings are resolved throughout the system

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/actionState.ts | Added type guards for `result.values` and `workingMemory` before calling `Object.keys()` to prevent runtime errors when these values are null/undefined |
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/relationships.ts | Added null/undefined check in `formatMetadata()` to return `'{}'` when metadata is null/undefined instead of crashing on `Object.entries()` |
| packages/core/src/runtime.ts | Added request context lookup in `getSetting()` for per-entity settings support - enables multi-tenant deployments with per-user API keys |
| packages/server/src/services/message.ts | Refactored message creation to emit MESSAGE_SENT event after successfully sending to central server, improving event lifecycle tracking |
| packages/core/src/request-context.ts | New file implementing request context system for per-entity settings in multi-tenant deployments |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant MessageService
    participant Runtime
    participant Provider
    participant Database

    Note over User,Database: Object.entries Error Flow (Before Fix)
    User->>MessageService: Send message
    MessageService->>Runtime: Process message
    Runtime->>Provider: Get context (actionStateProvider)
    Provider->>Provider: Access result.values (null)
    Provider->>Provider: Object.entries(null) ❌
    Provider-->>Runtime: CRASH

    Note over User,Database: Fixed Flow (After This PR)
    User->>MessageService: Send message
    MessageService->>Runtime: Process message
    Runtime->>Provider: Get context (actionStateProvider)
    Provider->>Provider: Check if result.values is object
    alt result.values is null/undefined
        Provider->>Provider: Skip Object.entries
    else result.values is valid object
        Provider->>Provider: Object.entries(result.values) ✓
    end
    Provider-->>Runtime: Return formatted context
    Runtime->>Runtime: Generate response
    Runtime->>Database: Create memory
    MessageService->>MessageService: Emit MESSAGE_SENT event
    MessageService-->>User: Response delivered

    Note over User,Database: Request Context Feature (New)
    User->>Runtime: getSetting(key)
    Runtime->>Runtime: Check request context
    alt Entity-specific setting exists
        Runtime-->>User: Return entity setting
    else No entity setting
        Runtime->>Runtime: Fall back to agent setting
        Runtime-->>User: Return agent setting
    end
```

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


Introduces `dynamicPromptExecFromState()` across Python, Rust, and TypeScript runtimes to provide schema-driven prompt execution with context validation via UUID codes. The implementation detects when LLMs truncate output due to limited context windows by injecting validation codes at strategic positions (start/middle/end or per-field). Supports four validation levels (0=trusted to 3=full), exponential backoff retries, and optional validation-aware streaming via `ValidationStreamExtractor`.

**Key changes:**
- Cross-language API consistency for dynamic prompt execution with state injection
- Validation code system to detect context overflow (4 levels: trusted, progressive, checkpoint, full)
- Streaming integration with progressive validation and retry support
- Schema-based structured output parsing (XML/JSON) with required field validation
- Performance metrics tracking per model+schema combination (TypeScript only)
- Comprehensive type definitions (`SchemaRow`, `RetryBackoffConfig`, `StreamEvent`)

**Critical issues in Python implementation:**
- Callable prompt invocation wraps state incorrectly (`{"state": state}` vs direct state access)
- Template substitution assumes `state.values` has dynamic attributes accessible via `dir()`, incompatible with protobuf State
- XML parsing regex `\w+` won't match validation field names with underscores like `code_text_start`

**Minor issues:**
- Rust template rendering uses basic string replacement instead of full Handlebars compiler
- TypeScript `_smartRetryContext` deletion during retry loop prevents reuse on subsequent attempts
- ValidationStreamExtractor abort handling may leave inconsistent state

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>


- Python implementation has runtime errors that will break production usage; TypeScript and Rust implementations are safer but need testing
- Score reflects critical logical errors in Python (3 bugs that will cause runtime failures), plus architecture differences across languages. TypeScript implementation is most complete with metrics and full Handlebars support. Python bugs must be fixed before merge to avoid breaking callers.
- `packages/python/elizaos/runtime.py` requires immediate fixes for callable invocation, state.values access pattern, and XML regex. Test the Python implementation thoroughly before merging.

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/python/elizaos/runtime.py | Adds `dynamic_prompt_exec_from_state` with validation codes and retry logic; has critical bugs in callable invocation, state.values access, and XML parsing regex |
| packages/rust/src/runtime.rs | Implements `dynamic_prompt_exec_from_state` with validation and retry; template rendering is basic string replacement vs full Handlebars |
| packages/typescript/src/runtime.ts | Implements `dynamicPromptExecFromState` with metrics, streaming, and validation; minor issue with `_smartRetryContext` deletion timing |
| packages/typescript/src/utils/streaming.ts | Implements validation-aware streaming with multiple extractor types; minor state inconsistency on abort signal |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Client
    participant Runtime
    participant ValidationExtractor
    participant LLM
    participant Parser

    Client->>Runtime: dynamicPromptExecFromState(state, schema, options)
    
    Note over Runtime: Generate validation codes<br/>(UUID snippets)
    
    Runtime->>Runtime: Build extended schema<br/>with validation fields
    
    Runtime->>Runtime: Inject codes into prompt<br/>(initial, middle, end)
    
    Runtime->>Runtime: Compile template with<br/>Handlebars/state values
    
    alt Streaming enabled
        Runtime->>ValidationExtractor: Create extractor<br/>(level, schema, codes)
    end
    
    loop Retry attempts (0 to maxRetries)
        Runtime->>LLM: Generate text with prompt
        
        alt Streaming
            loop Stream chunks
                LLM-->>ValidationExtractor: chunk
                ValidationExtractor->>ValidationExtractor: Extract field content
                ValidationExtractor->>ValidationExtractor: Check per-field codes<br/>(level 0-1)
                ValidationExtractor-->>Client: Stream validated content
            end
        else Non-streaming
            LLM-->>Runtime: Complete response
        end
        
        Runtime->>Runtime: Clean response<br/>(remove <think> tags)
        
        Runtime->>Parser: Parse XML/JSON response
        Parser-->>Runtime: Parsed fields object
        
        Runtime->>Runtime: Normalize structured response
        
        alt Validation level 0-1
            loop For each field with code
                Runtime->>Runtime: Check start/end codes match
            end
        else Validation level 2-3
            Runtime->>Runtime: Check checkpoint codes<br/>(one_initial, one_middle, etc)
        end
        
        Runtime->>Runtime: Validate required fields<br/>are present and non-empty
        
        alt All validations pass
            alt Streaming (level 2-3)
                Runtime->>ValidationExtractor: flush()
                ValidationExtractor-->>Client: Buffered content
            end
            Runtime->>Runtime: Remove validation code fields
            Runtime->>Runtime: Update success metrics
            Runtime-->>Client: Return parsed response
        else Validation fails
            alt Has retries remaining
                Runtime->>Runtime: Calculate backoff delay
                Runtime->>Runtime: Wait for backoff
                Note over Runtime: Loop continues with retry
            else No retries left
                Runtime->>Runtime: Update failure metrics
                Runtime-->>Client: Return null
            end
        end
    end
```

Uses global AsyncLocalStorage state in Node, so regressions would show up as mis-scoped settings across concurrent requests if misused.

> **Overview**

>

>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR adds `RequestContext` infrastructure to enable per-entity settings in multi-tenant deployments, allowing multiple users to share a single agent runtime while maintaining isolated settings (API keys, OAuth tokens, etc.).

**Key Changes:**
- New `RequestContext` interface and `runWithRequestContext()` API for propagating per-request entity settings
- AsyncLocalStorage-based implementation for Node.js (`request-context.node.ts`) ensuring proper async context isolation
- Modified `runtime.getSetting()` to check request context first before falling back to character settings
- Comprehensive test coverage (28 tests) covering context isolation, concurrency, priority, and integration with runtime

**Design:**
- Follows OpenTelemetry ContextManager pattern (consistent with existing `streaming-context.ts`)
- NoopContextManager fallback ensures backward compatibility
- Entity settings are pre-decrypted, avoiding duplicate decryption
- Clear priority chain: entity settings → character settings → null

**Testing:**
- Unit tests verify context isolation across 10 concurrent requests
- Integration tests confirm proper `getSetting()` behavior and priority
- Edge cases covered: null vs undefined, nested contexts, error propagation

**Issue Found:**
- `package.json` version changed to local development version (`1.0.0-local.1768325621`) - must be reverted before merge

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram

        Runtime-->>Runtime: Check character.settings/secrets
        Note over Runtime: Apply decryption if needed
    end
    
    Runtime-->>Server: Complete message processing
    Server-->>User: Send response
    
    Note over ALS: Context automatically cleared after fn() completes
```

Prj X: https://x.com/AgentRLM21:T1dcb,## Summary
- Make all HTTP MCP connections lazy after schema fetch (connect → fetch schemas → disconnect → register lazy). `ensureConnected()` creates per-entity connections with correct API keys from request context.
- Add `MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS` check in `validate()` via `runtime.getSetting()` for per-user OAuth tool filtering. Fail-open when unset for CLI/non-cloud compatibility.
- Remove all `as any` casts; add `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` to transport union type.
- Make `getRequestContext` import optional with info/warn logging when unavailable (not in all `@elizaos/core` versions).

## Context
In the multi-tenant cloud environment, a shared `AgentRuntime` is cached per `agentId:orgId`. Previously, HTTP MCP connections were established at init with the first user's API key baked in, and `validate()` returned `true` for all lazy connections regardless of the user's OAuth connections. This allowed User B to invoke MCP tools using User A's OAuth tokens.

## Changes
- `src/service.ts` — HTTP connections now lazy after schema fetch; optional `getRequestContext` import with logging
- `src/actions/dynamic-tool-actions.ts` — `validate()` checks `MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS` from request context
- `src/types.ts` — `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` added to `McpConnection.transport` union
- `tests/dynamic-tool-actions.test.ts` — 3 new tests for MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS filtering (include, exclude, unset)

- [x] Biome clean on all changed files

- [ ] Verify CLI/standalone usage unaffected (MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS unset → all tools available)

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR addresses a critical multi-tenant security vulnerability where HTTP MCP connections shared state across users, allowing User B to invoke tools with User A's OAuth tokens.

**Key Changes:**
- Made HTTP MCP connections lazy after schema fetch (connect → fetch schemas → disconnect → register lazy) so `ensureConnected()` creates per-user connections with correct credentials from request context
- Added `MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS` validation check in `validate()` to filter tools based on user's OAuth connections (fail-open when unset for CLI compatibility)

- Improved type safety in `getSettings()` by explicitly handling all return types from `getSetting()`

**Tests:**

- All 207 tests pass

**Security Impact:**
This PR successfully eliminates the shared-state vulnerability that allowed cross-user OAuth token access in multi-tenant environments.

   ```bash
   cd examples/art
   python -m venv venv
   source venv/bin/activate
   ```
3. Install dependencies:
   ```bash
   pip install -e ".[dev,local]"
   ```
4. Run tests:
   ```bash
   pytest tests/
   ```
5. Run the training pipeline:
   ```bash
   elizaos-art-2048 pipeline --steps 50 --rollouts 8
   ```
   Or with the alternative command if the above fails:
   ```bash
   HF_HUB_ENABLE_HF_TRANSFER=0 elizaos-art-2048 pipeline --steps 50 --rollouts 8
   ```

```mermaid
        Backend-->>Trainer: training complete
        
        Trainer->>Trainer: _save_checkpoint()
    end
    
    Note over Trainer: Phase 3: Final Evaluation
    Trainer->>Trainer: evaluate(episodes)
    Trainer->>Trainer: _generate_report(results)
```

> [!NOTE]
> **Medium Risk**
> Changes MCP connection lifecycle and request headers, which can affect multi-tenant isolation and connectivity behavior if request context or URL-origin checks are misconfigured. Also shifts `@elizaos/core` to a peer dependency, which may impact consumers’ dependency resolution.
> 
> **Overview**
> Adds **per-user MCP HTTP connections** by keying connections with `serverName:entityId` (via `getRequestContext()`), while keeping `stdio` transports shared; `ensureConnected`, `callTool`, `readResource`, and `restartConnection` now resolve the appropriate user-specific connection.
> 
> For HTTP transports, automatically injects an `X-API-Key` header from runtime setting `ELIZAOS_API_KEY` when not already provided, but only for same-origin requests to avoid leaking credentials.
> 
> Bumps package version to `1.8.2` and updates packaging so `@elizaos/core` is a `peerDependency` (and dev dependency) rather than a runtime dependency.

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR enables per-user MCP server connections in multi-tenant environments by leveraging request context to create isolated connections per entity. It moves `@elizaos/core` to peerDependencies to ensure request context is shared, and implements dynamic API key injection from context into HTTP headers.

**Key Changes:**
- Per-user connection isolation: Connection keys now include entityId for HTTP/SSE transports (e.g., `serverName:entityId`)
- Dynamic API key injection: Reads `ELIZAOS_API_KEY` from request context and injects into `X-API-Key` header for same-origin requests
- Peer dependency change: Moved `@elizaos/core` to peerDependencies to share singleton request context

**Critical Issues Found:**
- Connection key logic fails for eagerly-connected servers: `getConnectionKey()` only checks `lazyConnections` to determine transport type, so eagerly-connected HTTP servers won't get per-user isolation
- `restartConnection` bug: Uses `getConnectionKey()` to get user-specific key but connection may exist under base key, causing disconnect/reconnect to target wrong connection
- Environment variable fallback risk: Origin check defaults to `http://localhost:3000` if env vars aren't set, which could cause issues in production

<h3>Confidence Score: 2/5</h3>

- This PR has critical logic bugs that will break connection management in production multi-tenant deployments
- The per-user isolation feature is well-intentioned but has multiple implementation bugs: getConnectionKey() fails for eagerly-connected HTTP servers, restartConnection() targets wrong connection keys, and environment variable handling is fragile. These issues will cause connection leaks and incorrect routing in production.
- src/service.ts requires immediate attention to fix connection key management logic

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| package.json | Version bump to 1.8.2 and moved `@elizaos/core` to peerDependencies to share request context |
| src/service.ts | Implements per-user MCP connections with dynamic API key injection; contains critical bugs in connection key management |

</details>

<!-- /greptile_comment -->27:T44a,## Summary
- Fixed `buildConversationContext` to use `state.values.recentMessages` instead of `state.data.recentMessages`
- Removed unused `runtime` parameter from `buildConversationContext` function

## Problem

The plugin was failing on cloud because it read from `state.data.recentMessages`, which only exists in the official bootstrap provider. The cloud provider uses a different structure (`state.data.messages`).

The correct path is `state.values.recentMessages` — a formatted string available on **both** cloud and official bootstrap providers.

## Test plan
- [ ] Deploy on cloud and verify CREATE_N8N_WORKFLOW action receives correct conversation context
- [ ] Verify the action generates workflow from actual user request, not confirmation message

* **Refactor**

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->28:Tf7c,This PR fixes #5216 - EventType.MESSAGE_SENT event not being emitted when agent responses are sent to the central server API.

## Problem

The `sendAgentResponseToBus` function in `packages/server/src/services/message.ts` sends agent responses to the central server API endpoint (`/api/messaging/submit`), but it does not emit the `EventType.MESSAGE_SENT` event.

This means that plugins with handlers for the `MESSAGE_SENT` event never get triggered when messages are submitted to the central channel.

## Solution

Added `EventType.MESSAGE_SENT` event emission after a successful response from the central server API.

## Changes

1. Added `EventType` import from `@elizaos/core`
2. Emit `MESSAGE_SENT` event with proper payload:
   - `runtime`: The agent runtime instance
   - `content`: The message content being sent
   - `roomId`: The room ID where the message was sent
   - `worldId`: The world ID
   - `userId`: The user ID (agent ID in this case)
   - `agentId`: The agent ID

## Testing

All existing tests pass (532 pass, 0 fail).

Closes #5216

<!-- /greptile_comment -->2a:T11a0,## Summary

This PR adds defensive null/undefined checks before Object.entries() calls in the @elizaos/core package's settings utilities to prevent runtime errors.

## Changes

### packages/core/src/settings.ts

Added null/undefined guards to the following functions:

1. saltWorldSettings() - Check worldSettings parameter before calling Object.entries()
2. unsaltWorldSettings() - Check worldSettings parameter before calling Object.entries()
3. encryptObjectValues() - Check obj parameter before calling Object.entries()
4. decryptObjectValues() - Check obj parameter before calling Object.entries()

## Problem Solved

These functions are called throughout the codebase with data from database queries and metadata that may be null or undefined. Without these checks, the code throws:

Object.entries requires that input parameter not be null or undefined

This causes providers and handlers to crash, leading to agents ignoring messages.

## Related Issues

- Complements PR #6470 (plugin-bootstrap null checks)
- Fixes same root cause in @elizaos/core package

## Testing

- Functions now return empty objects {} when input is null/undefined instead of crashing
- Maintains backward compatibility - valid inputs continue to work as before
- Safe for all existing call sites that may pass null/undefined values

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

- Adds defensive guards around `Object.entries()` usage in core settings utilities to prevent crashes when upstream data is `null`/`undefined`.
- Updates `saltWorldSettings`/`unsaltWorldSettings` to return `{}` on invalid input instead of throwing.
- Updates `encryptObjectValues`/`decryptObjectValues` similarly to avoid runtime errors when secrets/settings objects are missing.
- Change is localized to `packages/core/src/settings.ts` and affects how invalid inputs are handled (now silently coerced to empty objects).

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>

- Generally safe to merge, but the new guards change function contracts and may silently mask invalid inputs (arrays/functions) without updating types.
- The change is small and addresses a real runtime exception, but returning `{}` for non-record inputs without updating TypeScript signatures can hide bugs and produce surprising output for arrays (numeric keys).
- packages/core/src/settings.ts (input guards and type signatures)

<!-- /greptile_comment -->2f:Tc65,## Summary

- **Fix**: Add null check for `runtime.providers` in `providersProvider`
- **Impact**: Prevents `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'filter')`

## Problem

When `runtime.providers` is `null` or `undefined`, the code crashes when attempting to call `.filter()` on it:

```typescript
const allProviders = runtime.providers;  // Can be null/undefined
const dynamicProviders = allProviders.filter(...);  // CRASHES
```

## Solution

Added defensive null check:

```typescript
const allProviders = runtime.providers || [];  // Safe fallback
const dynamicProviders = allProviders.filter(...);  // Safe
```

## Testing

- ✅ Verified fix prevents crash when providers is null/undefined
- ✅ Verified normal operation when providers array exists

## Files Changed

- `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/providers.ts` - Line 23

---

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

- Keeps existing provider filtering behavior by treating missing provider lists as an empty array.
- Change is localized to `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/providers.ts` and affects how bootstrap providers are enumerated at runtime.

<h3>Confidence Score: 5/5</h3>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR attempts to release version `1.5.12-alpha.2` but introduces a critical versioning issue: it downgrades all packages from `1.7.3-alpha.1` (current `develop` branch) to `1.5.12-alpha.2`.

## Critical Issues

- **Version Downgrade**: The base branch is at `1.7.3-alpha.1`, and this PR would revert to `1.5.12-alpha.2`, violating semantic versioning principles
- **Duplicate Version Tag**: Version `1.5.12-alpha.2` already exists as git tag on commit `11f56236`, but this PR creates the same version on a different commit (`a9dcf5a1`) with different content
- **Publishing Conflict**: NPM will reject publishing an already-published version, causing the release process to fail
- **Missing Changes**: This PR appears to be based on an old commit (`f36c19d2` - "Rename project to ElizaOS in README") and is missing hundreds of commits from `develop`

## What Changed

All 18 package files had their version numbers changed from `1.7.3-alpha.1` to `1.5.12-alpha.2`, along with corresponding updates to `bun.lock`.

## Recommendation

This PR should be **rejected**. It appears to be an automated release commit that was created from an outdated branch or incorrect base. The release automation may need to be re-run against the current `develop` branch to create a proper version bump (e.g., `1.7.3-alpha.2` or `1.7.4-alpha.0`).

<h3>Confidence Score: 0/5</h3>

- This PR is unsafe to merge and will break package versioning and publishing
- Score of 0 reflects a critical version downgrade issue that would break the monorepo's version consistency, prevent successful NPM publishing, and create version conflicts. This appears to be an incorrectly generated automated release commit that must be rejected.
- All files require attention - specifically `lerna.json` and all `package.json` files have incorrect version downgrades that must not be merged

<!-- /greptile_comment -->33:T17ef,# Overall Project Weekly Summary (Feb 1 - 7, 2026)

This week, ElizaOS made significant strides toward the launch of the Eliza App MVP, focusing on connecting AI agents to everyday messaging platforms and hardening security for professional use. Beyond technical fixes, the community began a major strategic shift, moving away from complex "word-based" instructions toward more reliable "logic-based" systems to make agents smarter and more efficient.

## Executive Summary
The project successfully integrated major messaging services like Telegram, Discord, and SMS into a unified cloud environment while securing the `eliza.app` domain. By implementing new "multi-tenant" security features and expanding Web3 financial tools, ElizaOS is transitioning from an experimental framework into a robust, production-ready ecosystem for autonomous AI agents.

### Key Strategic Initiatives & Outcomes

**Expanding Connectivity and User Reach**
*Goal: To make AI agents accessible on the apps people use every day.*
*   The core team successfully deployed Discord, Telegram, and native iOS messaging integrations, allowing agents to communicate across multiple platforms simultaneously ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).
*   The project secured the `eliza.app` domain and finalized a new "poke-style" onboarding flow to make it easier for new users to get started ([elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza)).

**Hardening Security and Multi-User Privacy**
*Goal: To ensure that when multiple people use the same agent, their private data and API keys remain completely separate and secure.*
*   Introduced "Per-User Connections," which allows the system to safely inject individual API keys based on who is talking to the agent, preventing data leaks in shared environments ([elizaos-plugins/plugin-mcp](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-mcp)).
*   Implemented new transaction validation "guardrails" to ensure that agents performing financial tasks operate within safe, pre-defined limits ([elizaos-plugins/registry](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry)).

**Improving Reliability and Cost Transparency**
*Goal: To make agents more predictable in their actions and easier to manage financially.*
*   A new "Cost Evaluator" was proposed to track exactly how much money an agent is spending on AI processing (tokens) in real-time ([elizaos-plugins/registry](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry)).
*   The workflow system was updated with a "human-in-the-loop" feature, allowing users to review and approve an agent's plan before it executes complex tasks ([elizaos-plugins/plugin-n8n-workflow](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-n8n-workflow)).

**Rethinking Agent Intelligence (The "Biological Trap")**
*Goal: To reduce the "overhead" of long text prompts by using structured logic, making agents faster and more capable.*
*   Across multiple repositories, the community began a high-level debate on moving away from prose-heavy instructions toward logic-based synchronization, aiming to solve performance bottlenecks at the architectural level ([elizaos/elizaos.github.io](https://github.com/elizaos/elizaos.github.io)).

### Cross-Repository Coordination

**Unified Ecosystem Expansion**
The project demonstrated tight coordination between the core framework and the plugin ecosystem to prepare for the MVP launch.
*   **Workflow Integration**: The `elizaos.github.io` repository updated its tracking pipeline to include the `n8n-workflow` plugin, ensuring that automated task management is a first-class citizen in the ElizaOS documentation and examples ([elizaos/elizaos.github.io#241](https://github.com/elizaos/elizaos.github.io/pull/241)).
*   **Security Synchronization**: Security audits in the core `eliza` repository were synchronized with the Model Context Protocol (MCP) plugin updates to ensure that new multi-user privacy features were implemented consistently across the entire stack ([elizaos/eliza#6472](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6472)).

## Repository Spotlights

### elizaos/eliza
*   **Messaging Expansion**: Integrated Discord as an AWS service ([#6398](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6398)) and added native iOS messaging via Blue.io ([#6399](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6399)).
*   **Architectural Modularity**: Introduced `RequestContext` to allow the system to remember user-specific settings without messy code workarounds ([#6457](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/pull/6457)).
*   **Stability Fixes**: Resolved a critical issue where user settings were being ignored by the cloud, which previously caused API key conflicts ([#6451](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza/issues/6451)).

### elizaos-plugins/plugin-mcp
*   **User Isolation**: Implemented per-user connections for the Model Context Protocol, a vital step for keeping user data private in multi-tenant apps ([#23](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-mcp/pull/23)).
*   **State Management**: Began fixing "shared-state" bugs to prevent different users' data from accidentally mixing during agent interactions ([#24](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-mcp/pull/24)).

### elizaos-plugins/registry
*   **Web3 Utility**: Added new plugins for "Smart Money" signals on Base L2 ([#253](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/pull/253)) and an AI Agent Job Marketplace ([#255](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/pull/255)).
*   **Safety Guardrails**: Integrated the Proofgate plugin to validate agent transactions before they are sent to the blockchain ([#254](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry/pull/254)).

### elizaos-plugins/plugin-n8n-workflow
*   **User Confirmation**: Added an `awaitingUserInput` flag so agents pause for permission before running complex automated workflows ([#13](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-n8n-workflow/pull/13)).
*   **Reliability**: Improved how agents identify user intent and fixed errors in how they report if a task was successful or not ([#8](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-n8n-workflow/pull/8), **P2 - Iterative improvement**3e:T1d0e,#### **Description**

Currently, the `llm_judge` evaluator provides a binary `PASS`/`FAIL` outcome. This is effective for clear-cut cases but doesn't capture the nuance of Large Language Model (LLM) responses, which can often be partially correct, contain good information but have poor formatting, or be conceptually right but incomplete.

This ticket proposes an enhancement to the `llm_judge` evaluator to support a multi-level evaluation scale (e.g., `FAIL`, `PARTIAL PASS`, `PASS`). This will enable more granular and realistic testing of LLM behavior, providing more insightful feedback on an agent's performance. It allows scenario creators to define what constitutes a full pass versus a partial one, leading to more sophisticated agent evaluations.

#### **Acceptance Criteria**

1.  The `LLMJudgeEvaluationSchema` in `packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts` is updated to allow the `expected` field to be an array of strings, representing the possible evaluation levels (e.g., `['FAIL', 'PARTIAL', 'PASS']`).
2.  The `LLMJudgeEvaluator` in `packages/cli/src/scenarios/EvaluationEngine.ts` is refactored to instruct the LLM judge to respond with one of the provided evaluation levels.
3.  The `EvaluationResult` interface is updated to include the specific `level` (e.g., 'PARTIAL') returned by an evaluator, in addition to the overall `success` boolean.
4.  The `Reporter` class in `packages/cli/src/scenarios/Reporter.ts` is updated to display the evaluation level in its output, using distinct icons and colors for each level (e.g., ✅ PASS, 🟠 PARTIAL, ❌ FAIL).
5.  The final `judgment` logic in `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts` is enhanced to accommodate the new levels. For example, an `all_pass` strategy should require all evaluations to achieve the highest success level (e.g., 'PASS').
6.  The process exit code logic remains `0` for an overall scenario pass and `1` for a fail, based on the final judgment.

#### **Technical Approach**

**1. Update Scenario Schema (`schema.ts`)**

Modify the `LLMJudgeEvaluationSchema` to accept an array of strings for the `expected` field. This array defines the custom evaluation scale for the judge. The system prompt for the LLM will be dynamically constructed from this array.

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts
// ...
const LLMJudgeEvaluationSchema = BaseEvaluationSchema.extend({
  type: z.literal('llm_judge'),
  prompt: z.string(),
  // Change from z.string() to z.array(z.string()) to define the evaluation scale.
  // Default to a binary scale if not provided.
  expected: z.array(z.string()).optional().default(['FAIL', 'PASS']),
});
// ...
```

**2. Refactor Evaluation Engine (`EvaluationEngine.ts`)**

The `LLMJudgeEvaluator` must be updated to pass the new evaluation scale to the LLM. The general `EvaluationResult` type will also be updated to include the `level`.

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/EvaluationEngine.ts
// ...
export interface EvaluationResult {
    success: boolean; // True if not the lowest evaluation level
    level: string;    // The specific outcome, e.g., 'PASS', 'FAIL', 'PARTIAL'
    message: string;
}

interface Evaluator {
    // This method will now return the string level of the outcome.
    evaluate(runtime: IAgentRuntime, result: ScenarioResult): Promise<string>;
    getMessage(level: string): string;
    // Helper to get the expected levels
    getLevels(): string[];
}

class LLMJudgeEvaluator implements Evaluator {
    constructor(private prompt: string, private expected: string[]) {}

    async evaluate(runtime: IAgentRuntime, result: ScenarioResult): Promise<string> {
        const systemPrompt = `You are an AI assistant that judges the output of a command. Based on the prompt and the command output, respond with ONLY one of the following values: [${this.expected.join(', ')}].`;

        const llmResult = await runtime.useModel('TEXT_LARGE', {
            system: systemPrompt,
            messages: [{
                role: 'user',
                content: `Prompt: ${this.prompt}\nOutput: ${result.stdout}`
            }]
        });
        
        const response = llmResult.trim().toUpperCase();
        // Validate the LLM's response against the expected levels.
        if (this.expected.map(e => e.toUpperCase()).includes(response)) {
            return response;
        }
        // Default to the first (lowest) level if the LLM's response is invalid.
        return this.expected[0].toUpperCase();
    }
    
    getLevels(): string[] {
        return this.expected;
    }
    // ... getMessage remains similar ...
}

export class EvaluationEngine {
    // ...
    async run(runtime: IAgentRuntime, result: ScenarioResult): Promise<EvaluationResult[]> {
        const results: EvaluationResult[] = [];
        for (const evaluator of this.evaluators) {
            const level = await evaluator.evaluate(runtime, result);
            const levels = evaluator.getLevels();
            // "Success" is defined as any outcome that is not the lowest possible level.
            const success = level !== levels[0].toUpperCase();
            results.push({ success, level, message: evaluator.getMessage(level) });
        }
        return results;
    }
}
```

**3. Enhance Reporter (`Reporter.ts`)**

Update the reporter to handle and display the new evaluation levels with distinct formatting.

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/Reporter.ts
// ...
  public reportEvaluationResults(results: EvaluationResult[]) {
    // ...
    results.forEach(res => {
      let status;
      // Use a switch to handle different levels, with default for unknown levels.
      switch(res.level.toUpperCase()) {
        case 'PASS':
          status = chalk.green('✅ PASS');
          break;
        case 'PARTIAL':
        case 'PARTIAL PASS':
          status = chalk.yellow('🟠 PARTIAL');
          break;
        case 'FAIL':
        default:
          status = chalk.red('❌ FAIL');
          break;
      }
      console.log(`${status}: ${res.message}`);
    });
    // ...
  }
// ...
```

**4. Update Judgment Logic (`scenario.ts`)**

The logic for determining the final outcome must be updated to be aware of the different levels.

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts
// ...
// --- JUDGMENT ---
if (scenario.judgment?.pass?.all) {
    // Strictest strategy: all evaluations must be the highest possible level.
    finalStatus = evalResults.every(res => res.level === 'PASS'); // Assuming 'PASS' is the highest
}
// Potentially add new strategies here in the future, e.g., 'all_pass_or_partial'
// ...
```

#### **Testing Strategy**

1.  **Create a new scenario file**: `llm-judge-partial-pass.scenario.yaml`.
2.  **Define a multi-level evaluation**:
    ```yaml
    # ...
    evaluations:
      - type: llm_judge
        prompt: "Respond with the capital of France in a complete sentence."
        expected: ['FAIL', 'PARTIAL', 'PASS']
    judgment:
      pass:
        all: true # This will require a 'PASS' result
    ```
3.  **Run with an input that yields a partial pass** (e.g., `input: "echo 'Paris'"`).
    *   **Verify**: The reporter shows `🟠 PARTIAL`, the final status is `❌ FAIL`, and the exit code is `1`.
4.  **Run with an input that yields a full pass** (e.g., `input: "echo 'The capital of France is Paris.'"`).
    *   **Verify**: The reporter shows `✅ PASS`, the final status is `✅ PASS`, and the exit code is `0`.3f:T495,## Description

Investigate and resolve the 3-second MCP plugin initialization delay on Vercel due to WebSocket handshake requirements.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Understand root cause of 3-second delay
- [ ] Research options: Redis cache, Gateway pattern, or dedicated server
- [ ] Implement solution that keeps connection alive
- [ ] Test performance improvement

## Technical Notes

**From today's meeting (Ben):**

* MCP plugin takes 3 seconds to initialize on every message
* Caused by needing to establish WebSocket communication with server
* Vercel serverless functions don't maintain state between invocations
* Options discussed:
  1. Redis cache for connection state (but WebSocket still needs reconnect)
  2. Gateway pattern like Discord (dedicated server for stateful connections)
  3. Accept the delay for now, optimize later

**Shaw's guidance:**

* For any WebSocket service, use the Gateway pattern similar to Discord
* "It's not like everything has to be serverless" - just avoid idle pods doing nothing
* Gateway service can scale, handles WebSocket → turns into HTTP to agent

## Assignee

Ben

**P0 - Blocking developer productivity**44:T495,## Problem

As autonomous agents become more capable, they need the ability to transact with each other - pay for services, data, or collaboration. Currently there's no standard way for Eliza agents to:

- Hold and manage wallets (USD/crypto)
- Transfer funds to other agents
- Use escrow for task completion
- Track revenue and expenses

## Proposed Solution

We've been building [AURA Infra](https://nanilabs.io) - payment infrastructure specifically designed for AI agents. We'd love to contribute a plugin that enables:

1. **Agent Wallets** - Each Eliza agent gets a wallet with USD balance
2. **Instant Transfers** - Send/receive payments between agents (2.9% fee)
3. **Escrow System** - Lock funds until task completion, then auto-release
4. **Revenue Tracking** - Dashboard for agent earnings/spending

## Why This Matters

The agent economy is exploding. Moltbook has 1.5M+ agents, agent tokens are launching, and real money is flowing. Infrastructure providers will win.

## Demo

Try it now (no signup): https://nanilabs.io/playground.html
API Docs: https://api.nanilabs.io/docs

Happy to contribute a PR or discuss integration. What do you think?

* **Inefficiency**: Using brute force Max 4.6 for everything may not create the best code
* **Lack of Guidance**: No clear process for when to use which model
* **Model Strengths**: Each model has different strengths that we're not leveraging optimally

## Model Analysis Needed

### Max Mode (Opus 4.6)

* **When it helps**: Specific, narrow scope tasks where depth is needed
* **When it doesn't help**:
  * Broader scope tasks (goes beyond requirements)
  * Creates unnecessary amounts of code that needs to be trimmed back
  * Over-engineers solutions

### Normal Mode

* **Better for**: Making things functional without over-engineering
* **Advantage**: More focused, less unnecessary code

### Composer

* **Strengths**: Super fast
* **Considerations**: Might not be as smart, but there are instances where it performs better
* **Use cases**: Need to identify when Composer is optimal

### Other Models (Codex, etc.)

* **Planning**: Which model is best for planning tasks?
* **Execution**: Which model is best for code execution?
* **Bug Fixing**: Which model is best for debugging and fixing bugs?

## Deliverables

1. **Process Documentation**: Create a comprehensive guide on when to use which model
2. **Decision Framework**: Clear criteria for model selection based on task type
3. **Cost Optimization**: Strategies to reduce unnecessary Max 4.6 usage
4. **Best Practices Guide**: Latest and greatest practices for each model
5. **Team Education**: Educate the team on optimal model usage
6. **Iterative Improvement**: Process to refine and improve the guidance over time

## Success Metrics

* Reduced monthly costs on Max 4.6
* Improved code quality
* Faster development cycles
* Better understanding of model strengths/weaknesses across the team

* Brandon is the most experienced with these tools and very excited about this topic
* This should be an ongoing process that evolves as we learn more

- checkShouldRespond defaults to true but can be turned off for ChatGPT mode53:T1cd9,## Summary

Fixes critical runtime errors in `plugin-bootstrap` providers when metadata or values are null/undefined.

## Problem

The agent crashes with the error:
```
Object.entries requires that input parameter not be null or undefined
```

This occurs in several providers:
1. **relationshipsProvider**: When `entity.metadata` is null/undefined
2. **actionStateProvider**: When `result.values` or `workingMemory` are not objects
3. **recentMessagesProvider**: Similar issues with null values

## Solution

Added proper null/undefined checks before calling `Object.entries()`:

### 1. `src/providers/relationships.ts`
- Added null/undefined check in `formatMetadata()`
- Returns `'{}'` for null/undefined metadata instead of crashing

### 2. `src/providers/actionState.ts`  
- Added type check for `result.values` before calling `Object.entries()`
- Added type/null check for `workingMemory` before calling `Object.keys()`

## Changes

- **packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/relationships.ts**: Guard `formatMetadata()` against null metadata
- **packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/actionState.ts**: Add type guards for `result.values` and `workingMemory`

## Testing

1. Started ElizaOS agent
2. Sent messages via web interface (http://localhost:3000)
3. Verified no more `Object.entries` errors
4. Confirmed agent responds properly instead of showing IGNORE action

## Impact

- ✅ Prevents agent crashes when entities have null metadata
- ✅ Improves stability for action result processing
- ✅ Fixes "IGNORE" action issue when agent can't retrieve conversation context
- ✅ No breaking changes - only adds safety checks

Co-Authored-By: Claude Sonnet 4.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR fixes critical `Object.entries` runtime errors in plugin-bootstrap providers that caused agent crashes when metadata or values were null/undefined. However, **the PR contains significantly more changes than described in the title and description**.

## What's Actually in This PR

### 1. Plugin-Bootstrap Fixes (Matches PR Description)
- `actionState.ts`: Added type guards before `Object.entries()` on `result.values` and `workingMemory`
- `relationships.ts`: Added null check in `formatMetadata()` to prevent crashes

### 2. Major New Feature (Not Mentioned in PR Description)
- **Request Context System**: New per-entity settings infrastructure for multi-tenant deployments
  - Added `packages/core/src/request-context.ts` and `request-context.node.ts` (856+ lines)
  - Modified `runtime.ts` `getSetting()` to check request context first
  - Enables different users sharing the same runtime to have different API keys, OAuth tokens, etc.

### 3. Message Service Changes (Not Mentioned in PR Description)
- Refactored message creation logic in `message.ts`
- Added `MESSAGE_SENT` event emission after sending to central server

### 4. Version Bumps
- All packages bumped to `1.7.3-alpha.3`

## Concerns

The PR title says "fix: Add null/undefined checks" but this PR includes:
- A major architectural feature (request context system)
- Message service refactoring
- 30 files changed, 1107 insertions, 52 deletions

**This should have been split into separate PRs** for better review, testing, and rollback capability. The plugin-bootstrap fixes are straightforward and safe, but bundling them with a major new feature makes it difficult to:
- Review each change independently
- Test each feature in isolation
- Roll back if issues arise with one component

## Technical Review

The actual code changes are well-implemented:
- Null checks are correctly placed and handle edge cases
- Request context system follows AsyncLocalStorage patterns appropriately
- Message service changes maintain event emission order

The plugin-bootstrap fixes will definitely prevent the `Object.entries` crashes described in the PR.

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>

- This PR contains well-implemented code but has significant scope creep beyond its stated purpose
- Score of 3 reflects that while the code quality is good and the plugin-bootstrap fixes are safe, the PR includes undocumented major features (request context system, message service changes) that should have been separate PRs. This makes comprehensive testing difficult and increases risk. The PR description is misleading about the actual scope of changes.
- Pay close attention to `packages/core/src/runtime.ts` and `packages/core/src/request-context.ts` as these introduce a new architectural pattern for per-entity settings that affects how settings are resolved throughout the system

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/actionState.ts | Added type guards for `result.values` and `workingMemory` before calling `Object.keys()` to prevent runtime errors when these values are null/undefined |
| packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/providers/relationships.ts | Added null/undefined check in `formatMetadata()` to return `'{}'` when metadata is null/undefined instead of crashing on `Object.entries()` |
| packages/core/src/runtime.ts | Added request context lookup in `getSetting()` for per-entity settings support - enables multi-tenant deployments with per-user API keys |
| packages/server/src/services/message.ts | Refactored message creation to emit MESSAGE_SENT event after successfully sending to central server, improving event lifecycle tracking |
| packages/core/src/request-context.ts | New file implementing request context system for per-entity settings in multi-tenant deployments |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant MessageService
    participant Runtime
    participant Provider
    participant Database

    Note over User,Database: Object.entries Error Flow (Before Fix)
    User->>MessageService: Send message
    MessageService->>Runtime: Process message
    Runtime->>Provider: Get context (actionStateProvider)
    Provider->>Provider: Access result.values (null)
    Provider->>Provider: Object.entries(null) ❌
    Provider-->>Runtime: CRASH

    Note over User,Database: Fixed Flow (After This PR)
    User->>MessageService: Send message
    MessageService->>Runtime: Process message
    Runtime->>Provider: Get context (actionStateProvider)
    Provider->>Provider: Check if result.values is object
    alt result.values is null/undefined
        Provider->>Provider: Skip Object.entries
    else result.values is valid object
        Provider->>Provider: Object.entries(result.values) ✓
    end
    Provider-->>Runtime: Return formatted context
    Runtime->>Runtime: Generate response
    Runtime->>Database: Create memory
    MessageService->>MessageService: Emit MESSAGE_SENT event
    MessageService-->>User: Response delivered

    Note over User,Database: Request Context Feature (New)
    User->>Runtime: getSetting(key)
    Runtime->>Runtime: Check request context
    alt Entity-specific setting exists
        Runtime-->>User: Return entity setting
    else No entity setting
        Runtime->>Runtime: Fall back to agent setting
        Runtime-->>User: Return agent setting
    end
```

- **Update node catalog** via new `crawl-nodes` script
- **Cloud bridge credential_data flow**: plugin creates n8n credentials from OAuth tokens/API keys provided by cloud bridge, caches mapping in credential store
- **Integration availability check** (spec 09): three-tier pre-generation check — immediate block when no service nodes remain, LLM feasibility assessment when partial coverage exists, proceed when all integrations are available. Prevents generating workflows the platform can't run
- **Conversation context preservation**: `buildConversationContext` passes last 5 messages to generation, preventing context loss after errors or multi-turn interactions
- **Constrain generation to catalog**: LLM prompt now enforces strict usage of provided node types only

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


Introduces `dynamicPromptExecFromState()` across Python, Rust, and TypeScript runtimes to provide schema-driven prompt execution with context validation via UUID codes. The implementation detects when LLMs truncate output due to limited context windows by injecting validation codes at strategic positions (start/middle/end or per-field). Supports four validation levels (0=trusted to 3=full), exponential backoff retries, and optional validation-aware streaming via `ValidationStreamExtractor`.

**Key changes:**
- Cross-language API consistency for dynamic prompt execution with state injection
- Validation code system to detect context overflow (4 levels: trusted, progressive, checkpoint, full)
- Streaming integration with progressive validation and retry support
- Schema-based structured output parsing (XML/JSON) with required field validation
- Performance metrics tracking per model+schema combination (TypeScript only)
- Comprehensive type definitions (`SchemaRow`, `RetryBackoffConfig`, `StreamEvent`)

**Critical issues in Python implementation:**
- Callable prompt invocation wraps state incorrectly (`{"state": state}` vs direct state access)
- Template substitution assumes `state.values` has dynamic attributes accessible via `dir()`, incompatible with protobuf State
- XML parsing regex `\w+` won't match validation field names with underscores like `code_text_start`

**Minor issues:**
- Rust template rendering uses basic string replacement instead of full Handlebars compiler
- TypeScript `_smartRetryContext` deletion during retry loop prevents reuse on subsequent attempts
- ValidationStreamExtractor abort handling may leave inconsistent state

<h3>Confidence Score: 3/5</h3>


- Python implementation has runtime errors that will break production usage; TypeScript and Rust implementations are safer but need testing
- Score reflects critical logical errors in Python (3 bugs that will cause runtime failures), plus architecture differences across languages. TypeScript implementation is most complete with metrics and full Handlebars support. Python bugs must be fixed before merge to avoid breaking callers.
- `packages/python/elizaos/runtime.py` requires immediate fixes for callable invocation, state.values access pattern, and XML regex. Test the Python implementation thoroughly before merging.

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Client
    participant Runtime
    participant ValidationExtractor
    participant LLM
    participant Parser

    Client->>Runtime: dynamicPromptExecFromState(state, schema, options)
    
    Note over Runtime: Generate validation codes<br/>(UUID snippets)
    
    Runtime->>Runtime: Build extended schema<br/>with validation fields
    
    Runtime->>Runtime: Inject codes into prompt<br/>(initial, middle, end)
    
    Runtime->>Runtime: Compile template with<br/>Handlebars/state values
    
    alt Streaming enabled
        Runtime->>ValidationExtractor: Create extractor<br/>(level, schema, codes)
    end
    
    loop Retry attempts (0 to maxRetries)
        Runtime->>LLM: Generate text with prompt
        
        alt Streaming
            loop Stream chunks
                LLM-->>ValidationExtractor: chunk
                ValidationExtractor->>ValidationExtractor: Extract field content
                ValidationExtractor->>ValidationExtractor: Check per-field codes<br/>(level 0-1)
                ValidationExtractor-->>Client: Stream validated content
            end
        else Non-streaming
            LLM-->>Runtime: Complete response
        end
        
        Runtime->>Runtime: Clean response<br/>(remove <think> tags)
        
        Runtime->>Parser: Parse XML/JSON response
        Parser-->>Runtime: Parsed fields object
        
        Runtime->>Runtime: Normalize structured response
        
        alt Validation level 0-1
            loop For each field with code
                Runtime->>Runtime: Check start/end codes match
            end
        else Validation level 2-3
            Runtime->>Runtime: Check checkpoint codes<br/>(one_initial, one_middle, etc)
        end
        
        Runtime->>Runtime: Validate required fields<br/>are present and non-empty
        
        alt All validations pass
            alt Streaming (level 2-3)
                Runtime->>ValidationExtractor: flush()
                ValidationExtractor-->>Client: Buffered content
            end
            Runtime->>Runtime: Remove validation code fields
            Runtime->>Runtime: Update success metrics
            Runtime-->>Client: Return parsed response
        else Validation fails
            alt Has retries remaining
                Runtime->>Runtime: Calculate backoff delay
                Runtime->>Runtime: Wait for backoff
                Note over Runtime: Loop continues with retry
            else No retries left
                Runtime->>Runtime: Update failure metrics
                Runtime-->>Client: Return null
            end
        end
    end
```

>

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>

This PR adds `RequestContext` infrastructure to enable per-entity settings in multi-tenant deployments, allowing multiple users to share a single agent runtime while maintaining isolated settings (API keys, OAuth tokens, etc.).

- Follows OpenTelemetry ContextManager pattern (consistent with existing `streaming-context.ts`)
- NoopContextManager fallback ensures backward compatibility
- Entity settings are pre-decrypted, avoiding duplicate decryption
- Clear priority chain: entity settings → character settings → null

- Integration tests confirm proper `getSetting()` behavior and priority
- Edge cases covered: null vs undefined, nested contexts, error propagation

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram

    Runtime->>RC: getRequestContext()
    RC->>ALS: storage.getStore()
    ALS-->>RC: Return active context
    RC-->>Runtime: Return context

        Runtime-->>Runtime: Return entitySettings.get(key)
        Note over Runtime: Pre-decrypted, return as-is
    else Fallback to character settings
        Runtime-->>Runtime: Check character.settings/secrets
        Note over Runtime: Apply decryption if needed
    end
    
    Runtime-->>Server: Complete message processing
    Server-->>User: Send response
    
    Note over ALS: Context automatically cleared after fn() completes

```

# Risks

Low

- Provider is opt-in and not registered by default
- No existing code paths are modified unless the RLM provider is explicitly used
- Fallback stub behavior when RLM dependency is not installed

# Background

## What does this PR do?


This PR introduces a **prototype RLM provider** for the Eliza Python core.

It adds:
- A minimal `RLMProvider` that follows the existing provider interface
- A thin `RLMClient` adapter that wraps an external Recursive Language Model (RLM)
- Safe stub behavior when the RLM dependency is not present

The goal is to explore whether RLM can be integrated cleanly as a reasoning backend,
without affecting Eliza’s memory, planning, or autonomy layers.

This is intentionally an early draft meant for discussion and iteration.

## What kind of change is this?

Features (non-breaking, opt-in)

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

Idea originated from this tweet https://x.com/shawmakesmagic/status/2013151880810057772?s=20

This PR is an experiment to see if RLM can fit naturally into the existing
provider/model boundary without increasing coupling or complexity.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

Prj X: https://x.com/AgentRLM5a:T1dcb,## Summary
- Make all HTTP MCP connections lazy after schema fetch (connect → fetch schemas → disconnect → register lazy). `ensureConnected()` creates per-entity connections with correct API keys from request context.
- Add `MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS` check in `validate()` via `runtime.getSetting()` for per-user OAuth tool filtering. Fail-open when unset for CLI/non-cloud compatibility.
- Remove all `as any` casts; add `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` to transport union type.
- Make `getRequestContext` import optional with info/warn logging when unavailable (not in all `@elizaos/core` versions).

## Context
In the multi-tenant cloud environment, a shared `AgentRuntime` is cached per `agentId:orgId`. Previously, HTTP MCP connections were established at init with the first user's API key baked in, and `validate()` returned `true` for all lazy connections regardless of the user's OAuth connections. This allowed User B to invoke MCP tools using User A's OAuth tokens.

## Changes
- `src/service.ts` — HTTP connections now lazy after schema fetch; optional `getRequestContext` import with logging
- `src/actions/dynamic-tool-actions.ts` — `validate()` checks `MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS` from request context
- `src/types.ts` — `StreamableHTTPClientTransport` added to `McpConnection.transport` union
- `tests/dynamic-tool-actions.test.ts` — 3 new tests for MCP_ENABLED_SERVERS filtering (include, exclude, unset)

**Key Changes:**
- Added fine-tuning dependencies (`peft`, `unsloth`, `trl`) to support parameter-efficient training

**Technical Notes:**
- The trainer now accesses private methods (`_prepare_backend_for_training`, `_train_model`) from the `art` library, which may be fragile if the library's internal API changes

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant CLI as CLI (cli.py)
    participant Trainer as GRPOTrainer
    participant Model as TrainableModel
    participant Backend as LocalBackend
    participant Env as Game2048Environment
    participant Agent as Game2048Agent
    participant RULER as RulerScorer
    
    CLI->>Trainer: pipeline(steps, eval_episodes)
    Trainer->>Trainer: initialize()
    Trainer->>Model: art.TrainableModel(name, base_model, project)
    Trainer->>Backend: art.local.LocalBackend()
    Trainer->>Model: register(backend)
    Trainer->>Backend: _prepare_backend_for_training(model)
    Backend-->>Trainer: base_url, api_key
    Trainer->>Model: Set inference_base_url and inference_api_key
    
    Note over Trainer: Phase 1: Baseline Evaluation
    Trainer->>Trainer: evaluate(episodes)
    
    Note over Trainer: Phase 2: Training Loop
    loop For each training step
        Trainer->>Trainer: train_step()
        Trainer->>Trainer: gather_trajectory_groups()
        
        loop For each group
            loop For each rollout
                Trainer->>Env: reset(seed)
                Env-->>Trainer: state
                
                loop While not done
                    Trainer->>Agent: format_action_prompt(state)
                    Trainer->>Model: openai_client()
                    Model-->>Trainer: client
                    Trainer->>Model: client.chat.completions.create()
                    Model-->>Trainer: response
                    Trainer->>Agent: parse_action(response)
                    Trainer->>Env: step(action)
                    Env-->>Trainer: new_state, reward, done
                end
                
                Trainer->>Trainer: Create Trajectory
            end
        end
        
        Trainer->>RULER: score_group(trajectory_group)
        RULER-->>Trainer: scored_group
        
        Trainer->>Backend: _train_model(model, scored_groups, config)
        Backend-->>Trainer: training complete
        
        Trainer->>Trainer: _save_checkpoint()
    end
    
    Note over Trainer: Phase 3: Final Evaluation
    Trainer->>Trainer: evaluate(episodes)
    Trainer->>Trainer: _generate_report(results)
    Trainer-->>CLI: Pipeline results
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->5f:T15b8,<!-- CURSOR_SUMMARY -->
> [!NOTE]
> **Medium Risk**
> Changes MCP connection lifecycle and request headers, which can affect multi-tenant isolation and connectivity behavior if request context or URL-origin checks are misconfigured. Also shifts `@elizaos/core` to a peer dependency, which may impact consumers’ dependency resolution.
> 
> **Overview**
> Adds **per-user MCP HTTP connections** by keying connections with `serverName:entityId` (via `getRequestContext()`), while keeping `stdio` transports shared; `ensureConnected`, `callTool`, `readResource`, and `restartConnection` now resolve the appropriate user-specific connection.
> 
> For HTTP transports, automatically injects an `X-API-Key` header from runtime setting `ELIZAOS_API_KEY` when not already provided, but only for same-origin requests to avoid leaking credentials.
> 
> Bumps package version to `1.8.2` and updates packaging so `@elizaos/core` is a `peerDependency` (and dev dependency) rather than a runtime dependency.
>
